# Calculate last week's date range (Monday to Sunday)
last_week_info = Broker.CALCULATE(
    # Get current date, go to start of this week (Monday), then subtract 7 days for last Monday
    last_monday = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-7 days'),
    # Get current date, go to start of this week (Monday), then subtract 1 day for last Sunday
    last_sunday = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 days')
)

# Filter USA customers and their transactions from last week
result = last_week_info.Customers.WHERE(
    country == "USA"
).transactions_made.WHERE(
    (date_time >= last_monday) & (date_time <= last_sunday)
).CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    total_amount = SUM(amount)
).SINGULAR()

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by claude:
# Calculate transactions in the last 8 weeks excluding current week
# First, get all transactions and calculate week information
transaction_info = Transactions.CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the start of the week for each transaction (Monday)
    week_start = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Check if transaction occurred on weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=7 when week starts on Monday)
    is_weekend = ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(date_time), (6, 7))
)

# Filter to last 8 weeks excluding current week
# Current week start is the most recent Monday
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
eight_weeks_ago = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-56 days')  # 8 weeks * 7 days

filtered_transactions = transaction_info.WHERE(
    (week_start >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (week_start < current_week_start)
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="weeks", 
    by=week_start
).CALCULATE(
    week_start = week_start,
    total_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions = SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(weekend_flag = IFF(is_weekend, 1, 0)).weekend_flag)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q4] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the time boundaries for the last 8 weeks, excluding the current week.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_period = DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-8 weeks')

# Filter transactions to include only stocks within the specified time frame.
transactions_in_period = Transactions.WHERE(
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= start_of_period) &
    (date_time < start_of_current_week)
)

# Calculate the start of the week for each transaction and identify if it occurred on a weekend.
transactions_with_week_info = transactions_in_period.CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ('Saturday', 'Sunday'))
)

# Group transactions by the start of the week and calculate the total and weekend transaction counts.
weekly_stock_transactions = transactions_with_week_info.PARTITION(
    name='weekly_groups',
    by=week_start_date
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Get transactions from the last 8 weeks excluding current week
# First, calculate the start of the current week (Monday)
current_week_start = Broker.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
).Transactions.CALCULATE(
    # Calculate week start for each transaction (Monday-based)
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Get day of week (1=Monday, 7=Sunday based on start_of_week config)
    day_of_week=DAYOFWEEK(date_time),
    # Check if it's a weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=7)
    is_weekend=IFF((day_of_week == 6) | (day_of_week == 7), 1, 0)
).WHERE(
    # Filter for last 8 weeks excluding current week
    (DATEDIFF('weeks', week_start, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')) > 0) &
    (DATEDIFF('weeks', week_start, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')) <= 8)
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = current_week_start.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", 
    by=week_start
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.DESC())

# First, filter transactions for stocks within the last 8 weeks (excluding the current one)
# and calculate the week's start date and a weekend flag for each transaction.
transactions_in_range = Transactions.WHERE(
    (ticker.ticker_type == 'stock') &
    (date_time >= DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')) &
    (date_time < DATETIME('now', 'start of week'))
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ('Saturday', 'Sunday'))
)

# Partition the filtered transactions by the week's start date and calculate the total
# and weekend transaction counts for each week.
result = transactions_in_range.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups",
    by=week_start_date
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_transactions=COUNT(transactions_in_range),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(transactions_in_range.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Calculate transactions in the last 8 weeks excluding current week
# First, get all transactions and calculate week information
transaction_info = Transactions.CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the start of the week for each transaction (Monday)
    week_start = DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    # Check if transaction occurred on weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=7 when week starts on Monday)
    is_weekend = ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(date_time), (6, 7))
)

# Filter to last 8 weeks excluding current week
# Current week start is the most recent Monday
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
eight_weeks_ago = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-56 days')  # 8 weeks * 7 days

filtered_transactions = transaction_info.WHERE(
    (week_start >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (week_start < current_week_start)
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="weeks", 
    by=week_start
).CALCULATE(
    week_start = week_start,
    total_transactions = COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions = SUM(Transactions.CALCULATE(weekend_flag = IFF(is_weekend, 1, 0)).weekend_flag)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start and end dates for the analysis period.
# The period starts 6 months before the beginning of the current month
# and ends at the beginning of the current month.
start_of_current_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
start_of_period = DATETIME(start_of_current_month, '-6 months')

# Filter customers who joined within the specified period and create a 'signup_month'
# field in 'YYYY-MM' format to be used for grouping.
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= start_of_period) & (join_date < start_of_current_month)
).CALCULATE(
    signup_month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, '0'))
)

# Partition the filtered customers by their signup month.
# Then, for each month, calculate the total number of signups (PMCS) and the
# average transaction amount (PMAT) for all transactions made by those customers.
result = recent_customers.PARTITION(
    name='monthly_groups', by=signup_month
).CALCULATE(
    month=signup_month,
    PMCS=COUNT(Customers),
    PMAT=AVG(Customers.transactions_made.amount)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only 'buy' types within the last 10 days.
# We use DATEDIFF to compare the transaction's date_time with the current time.
recent_buy_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "buy") &
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 10)
)

# Step 2: Create a new field for the ticker symbol to use it as a partition key.
transactions_with_symbol = recent_buy_transactions.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol
)

# Step 3: Partition the filtered transactions by their ticker symbol to group them.
ticker_groups = transactions_with_symbol.PARTITION(
    name="ticker_groups", by=(ticker_symbol)
)

# Step 4: For each ticker group, calculate the number of transactions and select the top 2.
# The result is ordered in descending order based on the transaction count.
top_2_tickers = ticker_groups.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol=ticker_symbol,
    num_buy_transactions=COUNT(Transactions)
).TOP_K(2, by=num_buy_transactions.DESC())

# First, filter the DailyPrices to include only records from the last 7 days.
# DATEDIFF("days", date, 'now') < 7 ensures we get today (diff=0) and the 6 previous days.
recent_prices = DailyPrices.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", date, 'now') < 7)

# Next, create a new collection that includes the 'ticker_type' from the related Tickers collection.
# This is necessary so 'ticker_type' can be used as a key for partitioning.
prices_with_type = recent_prices.CALCULATE(
    ticker_type=ticker.ticker_type
)

# Finally, partition the filtered data by 'ticker_type' and calculate the average 'close' price for each group.
# The result will contain each ticker_type and its corresponding average closing price (acp).
acp_by_ticker_type = prices_with_type.PARTITION(name="type_groups", by=(ticker_type)).CALCULATE(
    ticker_type=ticker_type,
    acp=AVG(DailyPrices.close)
)

[DEBUG] [Q14] Running model claude (attempt 0)

[DEBUG] [Q14] Running model gemini (attempt 0)

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by claude:
# Step 1: Extract month and symbol from daily prices
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    symbol=ticker.symbol,
    close=close,
    high=high,
    low=low
)

# Step 2: Partition by symbol and month to calculate monthly statistics
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(
    name="symbol_month", 
    by=(symbol, month)
).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoMC using PREV window function
result = monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    momc=(avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="symbol")) / PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="symbol")
).ORDER_BY(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Pre-calculate symbol and a sortable month string ('YYYY-MM') for each daily price record.
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    symbol=ticker.symbol,
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Partition by the combination of symbol and month to calculate the monthly aggregates for each ticker.
# This creates a new collection where each row contains the stats for a specific ticker in a specific month.
ticker_months_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="ticker_month_groups", by=(symbol, month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the aggregated monthly stats by symbol. This creates the necessary hierarchical context
# to apply a window function ('PREV') that resets for each ticker.
symbol_groups = ticker_months_stats.PARTITION(name="symbols", by=(symbol))

# Step 4: Access the sub-collection of monthly stats within each symbol group.
# Then, use the PREV window function to get the average closing price from the previous month for the same ticker.
# - 'by=month.ASC()' ensures we look at the immediately preceding month.
# - 'per="symbols"' ensures the calculation is partitioned by the ticker symbol.
result_with_prev = symbol_groups.ticker_months_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    prev_avg_close=PREV(avg_close, by=month.ASC(), per="symbols")
)

# Step 5: In a final CALCULATE step, compute the Month-over-Month Change (MoMC) using the previously calculated 'prev_avg_close'.
# A new CALCULATE is required because a field created by a window function cannot be used in the same operation.
result = result_with_prev.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    momc=(avg_close - prev_avg_close) / prev_avg_close
)

[DEBUG] [Q13] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Augment the DailyPrices collection with the ticker symbol and date components for partitioning.
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    symbol=ticker.symbol,
    year=YEAR(date),
    month_num=MONTH(date)
)

# Step 2: Partition by symbol and month to calculate monthly aggregates (average close, max high, min low).
# This creates a new collection of monthly statistics.
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="monthly_data", by=(symbol, year, month_num)).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    year=year,
    month_num=month_num,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the monthly statistics by symbol. This creates groups of months for each ticker,
# which is necessary for the PREV window function to operate correctly.
ticker_groups = monthly_stats.PARTITION(name="ticker_groups", by=(symbol))

# Step 4: Within each ticker group, access the monthly data and use the PREV function to get the previous month's average closing price.
# The data is ordered by year and month to ensure PREV retrieves the correct value.
final_data = ticker_groups.monthly_data.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", year, month_num),
    average_closing_price=avg_close,
    highest_price=max_high,
    lowest_price=min_low,
    prev_month_avg_close=PREV(avg_close, by=(year.ASC(), month_num.ASC()))
)

# Step 5: Calculate the final Month-over-Month Change (MoMC) and select the desired fields for the output.
result = final_data.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    average_closing_price=average_closing_price,
    highest_price=highest_price,
    lowest_price=lowest_price,
    MoMC=(average_closing_price - prev_month_avg_close) / prev_month_avg_close
)

# Step 1: Extract month and symbol from daily prices
price_info = DailyPrices.CALCULATE(
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0")),
    symbol=ticker.symbol,
    close=close,
    high=high,
    low=low
)

# Step 2: Partition by symbol and month to calculate monthly statistics
monthly_stats = price_info.PARTITION(
    name="symbol_month", 
    by=(symbol, month)
).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoMC using PREV window function
result = monthly_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    momc=(avg_close - PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="symbol")) / PREV(avg_close, by=(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC()), per="symbol")
).ORDER_BY(symbol.ASC(), month.ASC())

    symbol=ticker.symbol,

)

# Step 2: Partition by the combination of symbol and month to calculate the monthly aggregates for each ticker.
# This creates a new collection where each row contains the stats for a specific ticker in a specific month.
ticker_months_stats = price_info.PARTITION(name="ticker_month_groups", by=(symbol, month)).CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=AVG(DailyPrices.close),
    max_high=MAX(DailyPrices.high),
    min_low=MIN(DailyPrices.low)
)

# Step 3: Partition the aggregated monthly stats by symbol. This creates the necessary hierarchical context
# to apply a window function ('PREV') that resets for each ticker.
symbol_groups = ticker_months_stats.PARTITION(name="symbols", by=(symbol))

# Step 4: Access the sub-collection of monthly stats within each symbol group.
# Then, use the PREV window function to get the average closing price from the previous month for the same ticker.
# - 'by=month.ASC()' ensures we look at the immediately preceding month.
# - 'per="symbols"' ensures the calculation is partitioned by the ticker symbol.
result_with_prev = symbol_groups.ticker_months_stats.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    prev_avg_close=PREV(avg_close, by=month.ASC(), per="symbols")
)

# Step 5: In a final CALCULATE step, compute the Month-over-Month Change (MoMC) using the previously calculated 'prev_avg_close'.
# A new CALCULATE is required because a field created by a window function cannot be used in the same operation.
result = result_with_prev.CALCULATE(
    symbol=symbol,
    month=month,
    avg_close=avg_close,
    max_high=max_high,
    min_low=min_low,
    momc=(avg_close - prev_avg_close) / prev_avg_close
)

# First, filter transactions to include only those from the last 30 days
# and calculate the customer's country for each transaction.
transactions_with_country = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
).CALCULATE(
    country=customer.country,
    amount=amount
)

# Next, partition the filtered transactions by country.
country_groups = transactions_with_country.PARTITION(
    name="countries", by='country'
)

# Then, calculate the number of transactions and the total transaction amount for each country.
country_summary = country_groups.CALCULATE(
    country=country,
    number_of_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount)
)

# Finally, select the top 5 countries based on the total transaction amount in descending order.
top_countries_by_amount = country_summary.TOP_K(5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC())

# First, filter transactions to include only those from the last 30 days
# and calculate the customer's country for each transaction.
transactions_with_country = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
).CALCULATE(
    country=customer.country,
    amount=amount
)

# Next, partition the filtered transactions by country.
country_groups = transactions_with_country.PARTITION(
    name="countries", by='country'
)

# Then, calculate the number of transactions and the total transaction amount for each country.
country_summary = country_groups.CALCULATE(
    country=country,
    number_of_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(Transactions.amount)
)

# Finally, select the top 5 countries based on the total transaction amount in descending order.
top_countries_by_amount = country_summary.TOP_K(5, by=total_transaction_amount.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 8-week window.
# The window ends at the beginning of the current week.
date_boundaries = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    start_of_current_week=DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
).CALCULATE(
    start_of_window=DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-8 weeks')
)

# 1. Access payments from sales with a price over $30,000.
# 2. Filter these payments to include only those within the last 8 weeks (excluding the current week).
# 3. For each payment, calculate the start date of its week and a flag indicating if it was on a weekend.
payments_in_window = date_boundaries.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_window) & (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (6, 7)) # Assumes Monday=1, ..., Saturday=6, Sunday=7
)

# Group the filtered payments by their week's start date.
# For each week, count the total number of payments and the number of weekend payments.
# Finally, sort the results by week in ascending order.
weekly_payments_summary = payments_in_window.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", by=(week_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Get high-value sales and their payments
high_value_sales = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000)
payments_for_high_value = high_value_sales.payment.CALCULATE(
    payment_date,
    # Calculate the week start (Monday) for each payment
    week_start = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Check if payment was on weekend (Saturday=6, Sunday=7 in DAYOFWEEK)
    is_weekend = ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (6, 7))
)

# Calculate the current week start to exclude it
current_week_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
)

# Filter payments to last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
recent_payments = current_week_info.PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (_id.ISIN(payments_for_high_value._id)) &
    (DATEDIFF('weeks', DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'), current_week_start) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF('weeks', DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'), current_week_start) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend = ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (6, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = recent_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week = week_start,
    total_payments_received = COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received = SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Define the relevant time period: the last 8 full weeks, excluding the current one.
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
end_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# 1. Start with payments, filter them by date, and calculate the start of the week for each payment.
#    This pre-calculation is necessary for the subsequent partitioning.
payments_in_period = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_period) & (payment_date < end_of_period)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    # Also filter for payments linked to sales over $30,000.
    is_high_value_sale = sale_record.sale_price > 30000
).WHERE(is_high_value_sale == True)

# 2. Partition the filtered payments by the start of the week.
weekly_groups = payments_in_period.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start)

# 3. For each weekly group, calculate the total payments and the count of weekend payments.
#    A weekend day is identified if DAYOFWEEK returns 6 (Saturday) or 7 (Sunday), assuming the week starts on Monday (1).
result = weekly_groups.CALCULATE(
    week = week_start,
    total_payments_received = COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received = COUNT(PaymentsReceived.WHERE(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) >= 6))
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Define the start of the current week (assuming Monday is the start) and the beginning of the 8-week period.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_period = DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-8 weeks')

# 1. Filter sales with a price over $30,000 and access their payments.
# 2. Filter these payments to include only those from the last 8 full weeks (excluding the current week).
# 3. For each payment, calculate the start date of the week it belongs to.
payments_in_period = Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_period) & (payment_date < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week')
)

# 4. Group the filtered payments by their week's start date.
# 5. For each week, calculate the total number of payments and the number of payments made on a weekend (Saturday or Sunday).
#    (Assuming DAYOFWEEK returns 6 for Saturday and 7 for Sunday when the week starts on Monday).
# 6. Order the results by week in ascending order.
weekly_payment_summary = payments_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", by=week_start_date
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received=COUNT(PaymentsReceived.WHERE(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) >= 6))
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

result = weekly_payment_summary

# First, define a contextless expression to get the latest inventory snapshot for each car.
# We use RANKING to find the most recent snapshot_date for each car and SINGULAR to treat it as a single record.
latest_snapshot = Cars.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="Cars") == 1
).SINGULAR()

# Filter for cars that have at least one sale record and are not in inventory according to their latest snapshot.
sold_cars_not_in_inventory = Cars.WHERE(
    (HAS(sale_records) == 1) & (latest_snapshot.is_in_inventory == False)
)

# Group the filtered cars by make and model, then calculate the maximum sale price for each group.
# Finally, order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = sold_cars_not_in_inventory.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_groups", by=(make, model)
).CALCULATE(
    make=make,
    model=model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.sale_records.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Define a contextless expression to get the latest inventory status for a car.
# It ranks snapshots per car by date, picks the latest one, and returns its 'is_in_inventory' status.
# SINGULAR() is used because RANKING == 1 ensures only one record per car.
latest_inventory_status = Cars.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="Cars") == 1
).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory

# Start with the Cars collection and filter for cars that meet two criteria:
# 1. They have at least one sale record.
# 2. They are no longer in inventory, according to their latest snapshot.
sold_and_out_of_stock_cars = Cars.WHERE(
    (HAS(sale_records) == 1) & (latest_inventory_status == False)
)

# Partition the filtered cars by make and model.
# For each group, calculate the make, model, and the maximum sale price from all associated sales.
# Finally, order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = sold_and_out_of_stock_cars.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_groups", by=(make, model)
).CALCULATE(
    make=make,
    model=model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.sale_records.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get all their sales and calculate the month
sales_info = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.CALCULATE(
    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, "start of month"),
    sale_price = sale_price,
    months_ago = DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME("now", "start of month"))
)

# Filter for last 6 months excluding current
recent_sales = sales_info.WHERE(
    (months_ago >= 1) & (months_ago <= 6)
)

# Partition by month and calculate metrics
result = recent_sales.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", 
    by=sale_month
).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month,
    pmsps = COUNT(sales_made),
    pmsr = DEFAULT_TO(SUM(sales_made.sale_price), 0)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

cars_not_in_inventory = sold_cars_with_status.WHERE(
    latest_inventory_status == False
)

# Group by make and model to find highest sale price for each combination
result = cars_not_in_inventory.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_groups", 
    by=(car_make, car_model)
).CALCULATE(
    make=car_make,
    model=car_model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.max_sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Define a contextless expression to get the latest inventory status for a car.
# It ranks snapshots per car by date, picks the latest one, and returns its 'is_in_inventory' status.
# SINGULAR() is used because RANKING == 1 ensures only one record per car.
latest_inventory_status = Cars.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), per="Cars") == 1
).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory

# Start with the Cars collection and filter for cars that meet two criteria:
# 1. They have at least one sale record.
# 2. They are no longer in inventory, according to their latest snapshot.
sold_and_out_of_stock_cars = Cars.WHERE(
    (HAS(sale_records) == 1) & (latest_inventory_status == False)
)

# Partition the filtered cars by make and model.
# For each group, calculate the make, model, and the maximum sale price from all associated sales.
# Finally, order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = sold_and_out_of_stock_cars.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_groups", by=(make, model)
).CALCULATE(
    make=make,
    model=model,
    highest_sale_price=MAX(Cars.sale_records.sale_price)
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Define the time window for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# The period starts 6 months prior to the first day of the current month.
start_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
# The period ends on the first day of the current month.
end_of_period = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Create a "scaffold" of all unique months within the target period.
# This ensures that all months in the range are included in the final output,
# even if there were no sales. We derive this from the main Sales table.
all_months_in_data = Sales.CALCULATE(
    month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month')
).PARTITION(name='months', by='month').CALCULATE(
    month=month
)

# Filter the scaffold to include only the months in our defined 6-month window.
target_months_scaffold = all_months_in_data.WHERE(
    (month >= start_of_period) & (month < end_of_period)
)

# Filter the salespersons to include only those hired between 2022 and 2023, inclusive.
salespersons_in_period = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# For each month in our scaffold, calculate the PMSPS and PMSR metrics.
# This is done by looking at the sales made by our filtered group of salespersons for each specific month.
result = target_months_scaffold.CALCULATE(
    month=month,
    # PMSPS: Count the sales from the filtered salespersons that fall in the current month.
    PMSPS=COUNT(
        salespersons_in_period.sales_made.WHERE(
            DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == month
        )
    ),
    # PMSR: Sum the sale_price for the same set of sales. Default to 0.0 if there are no sales.
    PMSR=DEFAULT_TO(
        SUM(
            salespersons_in_period.sales_made.WHERE(
                DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month') == month
            ).sale_price
        ),
        0.0
    )
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# We need to calculate the date range first
last_6_months_sales = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    six_months_ago = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
).Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
).sales_made.WHERE(
    (sale_date >= six_months_ago) & (sale_date < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(

    salesperson_id = salesperson_id,
    sale_price = sale_price

    name="month_salesperson", 
    by=(sale_month, salesperson_id)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month = sale_month,
    salesperson_id = salesperson_id,
    sales_count = COUNT(sales_made),
    sales_revenue = SUM(sales_made.sale_price)
)

# Now partition just by month to aggregate across all salespersons
result = monthly_metrics.PARTITION(
    name="months",
    by=sale_month
).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month,
    PMSPS = SUM(month_salesperson.sales_count),
    PMSR = SUM(month_salesperson.sales_revenue)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# First, create a contextless expression to filter for sales that occurred in 2023.
sales_2023 = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023)

# Use the top-level 'Dealership' collection to perform the aggregate calculations.
result = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the total revenue by summing the sale_price of all sales in 2023.
    total_revenue_val=SUM(sales_2023.sale_price),
    # Calculate the total cost by summing the cost of the cars associated with those sales.
    total_cost_val=SUM(sales_2023.car.cost)
).CALCULATE(
    # Apply the GPM formula using the calculated total revenue and total cost.
    gpm=(total_revenue_val - total_cost_val) / total_cost_val * 100
)

    HAS(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == False)) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make = make,
    car_model = model,
    # Get the date when the car went out of inventory
    out_of_inventory_date = inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == False).SINGULAR().snapshot_date,
    # Get the sale price if sold on the same date
    sale_price_same_day = sale_records.WHERE(
        sale_date == inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == False).SINGULAR().snapshot_date
    ).SINGULAR().sale_price
).WHERE(

)

    make = car_make,
    model = car_model,
    sale_price = sale_price_same_day

    current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    six_months_ago = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
).Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
).sales_made.WHERE(
    (sale_date >= six_months_ago) & (sale_date < current_month_start)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    salesperson_id = salesperson_id,
    sale_price = sale_price
)

# Partition by month and salesperson to get counts and revenue
monthly_metrics = last_6_months_sales.PARTITION(
    name="month_salesperson", 
    by=(sale_month, salesperson_id)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month = sale_month,
    salesperson_id = salesperson_id,
    sales_count = COUNT(sales_made),
    sales_revenue = SUM(sales_made.sale_price)
)

# Now partition just by month to aggregate across all salespersons
result = monthly_metrics.PARTITION(
    name="months",
    by=sale_month
).CALCULATE(
    month = sale_month,
    PMSPS = SUM(month_salesperson.sales_count),
    PMSR = SUM(month_salesperson.sales_revenue)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Step 1: Extract year and month from payments and calculate totals per month
payments_with_month = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    payment_year = YEAR(payment_date),
    payment_month = MONTH(payment_date),
    payment_amount = payment_amount
)

# Step 2: Partition by year and month to get monthly totals
monthly_totals = payments_with_month.PARTITION(
    name="months", 
    by=(payment_year, payment_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year = payment_year,
    month = payment_month,
    total_payments = SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Step 3: Calculate MoM change using PREV window function
result = monthly_totals.CALCULATE(
    year = year,
    month = month,
    total_payments = total_payments,
    prev_month_payments = PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0),
    mom_change = total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

# First, calculate the year and month for each payment.
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    payment_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    payment_month=MONTH(payment_date)
)

# Next, partition by year and month to aggregate the total payment amount for each month.
monthly_totals = payment_info.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", by=(payment_year, payment_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year=payment_year,
    month=payment_month,
    total_payments_received=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Finally, calculate the month-over-month change using the PREV function and format the output.
result = monthly_totals.CALCULATE(
    month_identifier=JOIN_STRINGS("-", year, LPAD(month, 2, "0")),
    total_payments_received=total_payments_received,
    mom_change=total_payments_received - PREV(total_payments_received, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=total_payments_received)
).ORDER_BY(month_identifier.ASC())

cars_sold_same_day = Cars.WHERE(

    car_make = make,

    # Get the sale price if sold on the same date

        sale_date == inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == False).SINGULAR().snapshot_date
    ).SINGULAR().sale_price
).WHERE(

)

# This is done by filtering the 'sales_made' subcollection for each salesperson.
recent_sales = sales_made.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 30)

# The main query starts with the 'Salespersons' collection.
# We calculate the required metrics for each salesperson and then select the top 5.
top_salespersons = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    first_name=first_name,
    last_name=last_name,
    # We count the number of sales using the 'recent_sales' filter.
    total_sales_count=COUNT(recent_sales),
    # We sum the 'sale_price' from the 'recent_sales' to get the total revenue.
    total_revenue_amount=SUM(recent_sales.sale_price)
).WHERE(
    # We filter out salespersons who had no sales in the specified period.
    total_sales_count > 0
).TOP_K(
    # Finally, we select the top 5 salespersons based on their sales count in descending order.
    5,
    by=total_sales_count.DESC()
)

# Calculate the start and end dates of the previous ISO week
# Start of current week (Monday)
current_week_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
# Start of previous week (Monday of previous week)
prev_week_start = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-7 days')
# End of previous week (Sunday)
prev_week_end = DATETIME(current_week_start, '-1 day')

# Filter payments to previous week and prepare for partitioning
payments_prev_week = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= prev_week_start) & (payment_date <= prev_week_end)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    payment_method=payment_method,
    payment_amount=payment_amount
)

# Partition by date and payment method to get daily totals
result = payments_prev_week.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date=payment_date,
    payment_method=payment_method,
    daily_total=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())

    sale_price=sale_price,
    quarter=IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 3, 1,
              IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 6, 2,
                IFF(MONTH(sale_date) <= 9, 3, 4))),
    customer_state=customer.state
)

sales_with_quarter_dates = sales_2023.CALCULATE(
    sale_price=sale_price,
    customer_state=customer_state,
    quarter_start=IFF(quarter == 1, "2023-01-01",
                    IFF(quarter == 2, "2023-04-01",
                      IFF(quarter == 3, "2023-07-01", "2023-10-01")))
)

end_of_previous_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-1 day')

# Filter payments received within the previous week

    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_week) & (payment_date <= end_of_previous_week)
)

)

    (DATEDIFF("months", start_dt, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) & 
    (DATEDIFF("months", start_dt, DATETIME("now")) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0")),

)

    name="months", 
    by=(treatment_month)
).CALCULATE(

    patient_count = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    biologic_patient_count = NDISTINCT(Treatments.WHERE(is_biologic).patient_id)
)

# Order by month descending to show most recent first
result = monthly_stats.ORDER_BY(month.DESC())

    (start_dt >= start_of_period) & (start_dt < start_of_this_month)
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(start_dt), MONTH(start_dt)),
    patient_id=patient_id,

)

    start_of_current_month=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    start_of_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-3 months')
)

# Filter treatments to the specified period and prepare necessary fields
treatments_in_period = time_info.Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= start_of_period) & (start_dt < start_of_current_month)
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, '0')),
    patient_id=patient_id,
    is_biologic=(drug.drug_type == 'biologic')
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by month
monthly_partitions = treatments_in_period.PARTITION(name="monthly_groups", by=(treatment_month))

# Calculate the required patient counts for each month
result = monthly_partitions.CALCULATE(
    month=treatment_month,
    patient_count=NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    biologic_treatment_count=NDISTINCT(Treatments.WHERE(is_biologic).patient_id)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Get treatments from the last 3 months (excluding current month)
# First, calculate the date range for the last 3 months
recent_treatments = Treatments.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", start_dt, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) & 
    (DATEDIFF("months", start_dt, DATETIME("now")) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0")),
    patient_id = patient_id,
    is_biologic = drug.drug_type == "biologic"
)

# Partition by month to count distinct patients
monthly_stats = recent_treatments.PARTITION(
    name="months", 
    by=(treatment_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month = treatment_month,
    patient_count = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    biologic_patient_count = NDISTINCT(Treatments.WHERE(is_biologic).patient_id)
)

# Order by month descending to show most recent first
result = monthly_stats.ORDER_BY(month.DESC())

[DEBUG] [Q69] Code generated by gemini:
# First, calculate the number of distinct drugs prescribed by each doctor.
# This creates a new collection containing the doctor's ID, specialty, and the calculated drug count.
doctor_drug_counts = Doctors.CALCULATE(
    doc_id=doc_id,
    speciality=speciality,
    num_distinct_drugs=NDISTINCT(prescribed_treatments.drug_id)
)

# Next, partition the results by specialty to group doctors.
# Then, for the doctors within each specialty group, calculate their rank (SDRSDR).
# The RANKING function operates within each partition, ranking doctors by the number of distinct drugs in descending order.
result = doctor_drug_counts.PARTITION(name="specialties", by=(speciality)).Doctors.CALCULATE(
    doc_id=doc_id,
    speciality=speciality,
    num_distinct_drugs=num_distinct_drugs,
    SDRSDR=RANKING(by=num_distinct_drugs.DESC())
)

    (DATEDIFF("months", start_dt, DATETIME("now")) <= 12)
).CALCULATE(

    pmpd = NDISTINCT(Treatments.CALCULATE(patient_diag = JOIN_STRINGS("-", patient_id, diag_id)).patient_diag)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

start_of_filter_window = DATETIME(start_of_current_month, '-12 months')

# Filter treatments to the specified window and create a 'year_month' field for grouping.
treatments_in_window = Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= start_of_filter_window) & (start_dt < start_of_current_month)
).CALCULATE(
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, '0'))
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by month and calculate the required metrics.
result = treatments_in_window.PARTITION(
    name='monthly_groups', by='year_month'
).CALCULATE(
    month=year_month,
    PMPD=NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    PMTC=COUNT(Treatments)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define the start of the current month and 12 months prior
start_of_this_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
start_of_12_months_prior = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-12 months')

# Filter treatments to include only those within the last 12 full months
treatments_in_range = Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= start_of_12_months_prior) & (start_dt < start_of_this_month)
)

# Create a formatted month string (yyyy-mm) to use as a partition key
treatments_with_month_key = treatments_in_range.CALCULATE(
    month_key=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0"))
)

# Partition by the month key and calculate the required metrics for each month
result = treatments_with_month_key.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups", by=(month_key)
).CALCULATE(
    month=month_key,
    PMPD=NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    PMTC=COUNT(Treatments)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q71] Code generated by claude:
# Get treatments from the last 12 months, excluding the current month
# First, calculate the start of the current month and go back 12 months
treatments_last_12_months = Treatments.WHERE(
    (start_dt >= DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-12 months')) &
    (start_dt < DATETIME('now', 'start of month'))
).CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = JOIN_STRINGS('-', YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, '0')),
    patient_id = patient_id,
    diag_id = diag_id
)

# Partition by month and calculate PMPD and PMTC
monthly_stats = treatments_last_12_months.PARTITION(
    name='months', 
    by=(treatment_month,)
).CALCULATE(
    month = treatment_month,
    # For PMPD, we need to count unique patient-diagnosis combinations
    # First create a partition by patient_id and diag_id to get unique combinations
    pmpd = COUNT(Treatments.PARTITION(name='patient_diag', by=(patient_id, diag_id))),
    # For PMTC, simply count all treatments in the month
    pmtc = COUNT(Treatments)
)

# Order by month
result = monthly_stats.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q74] Code generated by claude:
# Get patients with treatments and their first treatment year
patients_with_first_treatment = Patients.WHERE(
    HAS(treatments_received) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    patient_id,
    first_treatment_year = YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

# Group by year and count new patients
yearly_new_patients = patients_with_first_treatment.PARTITION(
    name="years", 
    by=first_treatment_year
).CALCULATE(
    year = first_treatment_year,
    num_new_patients = COUNT(Patients)
)

# Calculate NPI (increase from previous year)
result = yearly_new_patients.CALCULATE(
    year,
    number_of_new_patients = num_new_patients,
    npi = num_new_patients - PREV(num_new_patients, by=year.ASC(), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC())

# First, filter the 'Outcomes' to include only records where PASI scores for both day 7 and day 100 are present.
valid_outcomes = Outcomes.WHERE(PRESENT(day7_pasi_score) & PRESENT(day100_pasi_score))

# Next, calculate the average PASI scores for day 7 and day 100 from the filtered set of outcomes.
# This is done at the top-level 'DermTreatment' collection to get the overall averages.
average_scores = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    avg_d7_pasi=AVG(valid_outcomes.day7_pasi_score),
    avg_d100_pasi=AVG(valid_outcomes.day100_pasi_score)
)

# Finally, apply the D7D100PIR formula to the calculated averages to get the final percentage.
# A second CALCULATE is used to reference the fields created in the previous step.
result = average_scores.CALCULATE(
    d7d100pir=(average_scores.avg_d100_pasi - average_scores.avg_d7_pasi) / average_scores.avg_d7_pasi * 100.0
)

# First, calculate the number of relevant adverse events for each drug.
drug_adverse_events = Drugs.CALCULATE(
    drug_id=drug_id,
    drug_name=drug_name,
    # Count adverse events where the report date and treatment start date are in the same month and year.
    num_adverse_events=COUNT(
        treatments_used_in.adverse_events.WHERE(
            (YEAR(reported_dt) == YEAR(treatments_used_in.start_dt)) & 
            (MONTH(reported_dt) == MONTH(treatments_used_in.start_dt))
        )
    )
)

# Then, select the top drug based on the calculated count of adverse events.
result = drug_adverse_events.TOP_K(1, by=num_adverse_events.DESC())

# First, filter treatments to include only those that ended in the year 2022 and extract the patient's insurance type.
treatments_2022_info = Treatments.WHERE(YEAR(end_dt) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    insurance_type=patient.ins_type
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by insurance type to create groups for aggregation.
insurance_groups = treatments_2022_info.PARTITION(name="ins_groups", by=insurance_type)

# For each insurance type group, calculate the average day 100 PASI score and the number of distinct patients.
insurance_stats = insurance_groups.CALCULATE(
    ins_type=insurance_type,
    avg_pasi_score_100=AVG(Treatments.outcome_records.day100_pasi_score),
    distinct_patient_count=NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient.patient_id)
)

# Finally, select the top 5 insurance types with the lowest average PASI score.
result = insurance_stats.TOP_K(5, by=avg_pasi_score_100.ASC())

# First, prepare treatment data with year/month information
treatment_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_year = YEAR(start_dt),
    treatment_month = MONTH(start_dt),
    drug_id = drug_id
)

# For each treatment, count adverse events in the same month
treatments_with_counts = treatment_info.CALCULATE(
    drug_id = drug_id,
    same_month_adverse_events = COUNT(
        adverse_events.WHERE(
            (YEAR(reported_dt) == treatment_year) & 
            (MONTH(reported_dt) == treatment_month)
        )
    )
)

# Group by drug and sum the counts
drug_adverse_counts = treatments_with_counts.PARTITION(
    name="drug_groups", 
    by=drug_id
).CALCULATE(
    drug_id = drug_id,
    total_adverse_events = SUM(Treatments.same_month_adverse_events)
)

# Join with drug information and find the top drug
result = drug_adverse_counts.CALCULATE(
    drug_id = drug_id,
    drug_name = Drugs.WHERE(drug_id == drug_id).SINGULAR().drug_name,
    num_adverse_events = total_adverse_events
).TOP_K(1, by=num_adverse_events.DESC())

# First, filter treatments to include only those started in the past 6 months
# and calculate the doctor's specialty for each treatment.
recent_treatments_by_specialty = Treatments.WHERE(start_dt >= DATETIME('now', '-6 months')).CALCULATE(
    doc_specialty=doctor.speciality
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by specialty, then calculate the number of treatments
# and the total drug amount for each specialty. Finally, select the top 3 specialties
# based on the total drug amount.
top_3_specialties = recent_treatments_by_specialty.PARTITION(
    name="specialty_groups", by=doc_specialty
).CALCULATE(
    specialty=doc_specialty,
    number_of_treatments=COUNT(Treatments),
    total_drug_amount=SUM(Treatments.tot_drug_amt)
).TOP_K(3, by=total_drug_amount.DESC())

# Define a filter for doctors registered two years ago from the current date.
registration_year_filter = YEAR(DATETIME('now')) - 2

# Start with the Doctors collection and filter by the registration year.
# Then, access their prescribed treatments and use the RANKING window function
# to find the earliest treatment for each doctor based on the start date.
first_treatments = Doctors.WHERE(
    year_reg == registration_year_filter
).prescribed_treatments.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=start_dt.ASC(), per="Doctors") == 1
)

# Select the doctor's last name, registration year, and the ID and start date of their first treatment.
result = first_treatments.CALCULATE(
    last_name=doctor.last_name,
    year_reg=doctor.year_reg,
    first_treatment_id=treatment_id,
    first_treatment_date=start_dt
)

)

    # Filter for notifications from the last 3 weeks excluding current week
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME("now")) >= 1) & 
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME("now")) <= 3)
).CALCULATE(

)

    name="weekly_groups", 
    by=week_start
).CALCULATE(
    week = week_start,
    total_notifications = COUNT(notifications),
    weekend_notifications = SUM(notifications.CALCULATE(
        weekend_flag = IFF(is_weekend, 1, 0)

# Get the current date and calculate the date range for the last 3 weeks excluding current week
# We'll filter notifications from the last 3 weeks, excluding the current week
result = Users.WHERE(
    (country == "US") | (country == "Canada")
).notifications.CALCULATE(
    # Truncate created_at to the start of the week
    week_start = DATETIME(created_at, "start of week"),
    # Identify if the notification was sent on a weekend (Saturday=7, Sunday=1)
    is_weekend = (DAYOFWEEK(created_at) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(created_at) == 7)
).WHERE(
    # Filter for notifications from the last 3 weeks, excluding current week
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", week_start, DATETIME("now", "start of week")) > 0) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", week_start, DATETIME("now", "start of week")) <= 3)
).PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups", 
    by=(week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start = week_start,
    total_notifications = COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications = SUM(Notifications.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.DESC())

# Define the time boundaries for the last 3 weeks, excluding the current week.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_period = DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-3 weeks')

# Filter for notifications from users in the US or Canada within the specified timeframe.
relevant_notifications = Ewallet.Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ('US', 'Canada'))
).notifications.WHERE(
    (created_at >= start_of_period) & (created_at < start_of_current_week)
)

# Calculate the week and a weekend flag for each notification to prepare for partitioning.
notification_info = relevant_notifications.CALCULATE(
    notification_week=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYNAME(created_at), ('Saturday', 'Sunday'))
)

# Partition the notifications by week and then calculate the total and weekend counts for each week.
weekly_notification_counts = notification_info.PARTITION(name='weeks', by=(notification_week)).CALCULATE(
    week=notification_week,
    total_notifications=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications=SUM(Notifications.is_weekend)
)

# 1. The transaction must be sent by a user.
# 2. The user's country must be 'US'.

).Users.WHERE(

    by=week_start
).CALCULATE(
    week_start = week_start,
    total_notifications = COUNT(notifications),

# Define the start of the current week and the start of the 3-week window prior to it.
start_of_current_week = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
three_weeks_ago = DATETIME(start_of_current_week, '-3 weeks')

# Filter notifications for users in the US or Canada that were sent in the last three full weeks.
# Calculate the start of the week for each notification to be used for grouping.
filtered_notifications = Notifications.WHERE(
    ISIN(user.country, ("US", "Canada")) &
    (created_at >= three_weeks_ago) &
    (created_at < start_of_current_week)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start_date=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week')
)

# Partition the filtered notifications by week and calculate the total and weekend counts.
result = filtered_notifications.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_groups",
    by=(week_start_date,)
).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start_date,
    total_notifications=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications=COUNT(Notifications.WHERE(ISIN(DAYNAME(created_at), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))))
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

# Define the time range for the last two full months
start_date_range = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')
end_date_range = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Calculate Per Month Daily Active Users (PMDAU)
result = Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= start_date_range) &
    (created_at < end_date_range) &
    (sender_type == 0)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'),
    transaction_day=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of day')
).PARTITION(
    name="daily_users", by=(transaction_month, transaction_day)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month,
    dau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
).PARTITION(
    name="monthly_pmdau", by=transaction_month
).CALCULATE(
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(transaction_month), LPAD(MONTH(transaction_month), 2, "0")),
    pmdau=AVG(daily_users.dau)
)

[DEBUG] [Q102] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter transactions for sender_type 0 and the last two full months.
# It defines the time window from the start of two months ago to the start of the current month.
transactions_in_range = Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (created_at >= DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')) &
    (created_at < DATETIME('now', 'start of month'))
)

# Step 2: Extract date components to prepare for daily aggregation.
daily_transaction_info = transactions_in_range.CALCULATE(
    tx_year=YEAR(created_at),
    tx_month=MONTH(created_at),
    tx_day=DAY(created_at)
)

# Step 3: Partition by day to calculate the Daily Active Users (DAU).
# DAU is the number of distinct senders per day.
daily_active_users = daily_transaction_info.PARTITION(
    name="daily_groups",
    by=(tx_year, tx_month, tx_day)
).CALCULATE(
    tx_year=tx_year,
    tx_month=tx_month,
    dau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
)

# Step 4: Partition the daily stats by month to calculate the average DAU, which is the PMDAU.
# It also formats the month string as 'yyyy-mm'.
pmdau_by_month = daily_active_users.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups",
    by=(tx_year, tx_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month_str=JOIN_STRINGS('-', tx_year, LPAD(tx_month, 2, '0')),
    pmdau=AVG(daily_groups.dau)
)

# Step 5: Select the final fields for the output.
result = pmdau_by_month.CALCULATE(
    month=month_str,
    pmdau=pmdau
)

[DEBUG] [Q102] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the start and end dates for the analysis period.
# The period starts 2 months before the beginning of the current month and ends at the beginning of the current month.
start_of_current_month = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
start_of_period = DATETIME(start_of_current_month, '-2 months')

# 1. Filter transactions to the relevant time period and sender type.
# 2. Calculate the specific date (day) for each transaction to enable daily grouping.
transactions_in_period = Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= start_of_period) &
    (created_at < start_of_current_month) &
    (sender_type == 0)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_date=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of day')
)

# Group transactions by day to calculate the Daily Active Users (DAU).
# DAU is the number of distinct senders on a given day.
daily_active_users = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="daily_groups",
    by=(transaction_date)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_date,
    dau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
)

# Group the daily DAU stats by month to calculate the Per Month Daily Active Users (PMDAU).
# PMDAU is the average of the DAU values for each month.
# The month is formatted as 'yyyy-mm'.
pmdau_by_month = daily_active_users.CALCULATE(
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(transaction_date), LPAD(MONTH(transaction_date), 2, "0"))
).PARTITION(
    name="monthly_groups",
    by=(year_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month=year_month,
    pmdau=AVG(daily_groups.dau)
)

# Assign the final result to a variable.
result = pmdau_by_month

[DEBUG] [Q107] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the date boundaries for the previous month using the DATETIME function.
# 'current_month_start' is the first moment of the current month.
# 'previous_month_start' is the first moment of the last month.
current_month_start = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
previous_month_start = DATETIME(current_month_start, '-1 month')

# Filter the 'Transactions' collection to include only those from the previous month.
last_month_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= previous_month_start) & (created_at < current_month_start)
)

# Count the total number of transactions within the filtered period.
total_tx_count = COUNT(last_month_transactions)

# Count the number of successful transactions by filtering where the status is 'success'.
successful_tx_count = COUNT(last_month_transactions.WHERE(status == 'success'))

# Calculate the Success Transaction Rate (STR) for the previous month.
# The IFF function is used to prevent division by zero if there were no transactions, returning 0 in that case.
# We multiply by 1.0 to ensure the result is a floating-point number.
result = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    previous_month_str=IFF(
        total_tx_count > 0,
        successful_tx_count * 1.0 / total_tx_count,
        0
    )
)

merchant_coupon_counts = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_id=mid,
    merchant_name=name,
    merchant_creation_year=YEAR(created_at),
    merchant_creation_month=MONTH(created_at),
    num_coupons_in_same_month=COUNT(
        coupons.WHERE(
            (YEAR(created_at) == merchant_creation_year) & 
            (MONTH(created_at) == merchant_creation_month)
        )
    )
)

transactions_2023 = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(created_at) == 2023) & 
    (status == "successful") & 
    (sender_type == 1) &  # Assuming 1 represents users, not merchants
    (sending_user.status == "active")
).CALCULATE(

)

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only those sent by active users in 2023 that were successful.
successful_transactions_from_users = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(created_at) == 2023) &
    (status == 'completed') &
    (HAS(sending_user) == 1) &
    (sending_user.status == 'active')
)

# Step 2: Prepare the data for partitioning by calculating the start of the month for each transaction
# and extracting the sender's user ID.
transactions_with_month_info = successful_transactions_from_users.CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'),
    sender_user_id=sending_user.uid
)

# Step 3: Partition the transactions by month, then calculate the number of distinct users for each month
# and sort the results by date.
monthly_active_senders = transactions_with_month_info.PARTITION(
    name="transactions_by_month",
    by=(transaction_month)
).CALCULATE(
    month=transaction_month,
    num_distinct_active_users=NDISTINCT(transactions_with_month_info.sender_user_id)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

)

country_stats = users_with_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="countries", 
    by=(country,)
).CALCULATE(
    country=country,
    num_distinct_users=COUNT(Users),
    total_transaction_amount=SUM(Users.transactions_sent.WHERE(sender_type == 0).amount)
)

# First, find the earliest coupon for each merchant
merchants_with_earliest_coupon = Merchants.WHERE(HAS(coupons) == 1).CALCULATE(
    merchant_id=mid,
    registration_date=created_at,
    earliest_coupon=coupons.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=start_date.ASC(), per="Merchants") == 1
    ).SINGULAR()
)

# Filter for merchants whose earliest coupon was within a year of registration
result = merchants_with_earliest_coupon.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", registration_date, earliest_coupon.start_date) <= 365
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_id,
    registration_date,
    earliest_coupon_id=earliest_coupon.cid,
    earliest_coupon_start_date=earliest_coupon.start_date
)

    name="device_groups", 
    by=device_type
).CALCULATE(

# First, define a contextless expression to find the earliest coupon for each merchant.
# We use RANKING to identify the coupon with the minimum start_date for each merchant.
# SINGULAR() is used because we expect exactly one earliest coupon per merchant.
earliest_coupon = Merchants.coupons.WHERE(
    RANKING(by=start_date.ASC(), per="Merchants", allow_ties=False) == 1
).SINGULAR()

# Now, filter the merchants based on the time difference and select the required fields.
result = Merchants.WHERE(
    (HAS(coupons) == 1) & (DATEDIFF("days", created_at, earliest_coupon.start_date) <= 365)
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_id=mid,
    registration_date=created_at,
    earliest_coupon_id=earliest_coupon.cid,
    start_date=earliest_coupon.start_date
)
